=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer eventTime denoting the duration of an event, where the event occurs from time t = 0 to time t = eventTime.
You are also given two integer arrays startTime and endTime, each of length n. These represent the start and end time of n non-overlapping meetings, where the i^th meeting occurs during the time [startTime[i], endTime[i]].
You can reschedule at most k meetings by moving their start time while maintaining the same duration, to maximize the longest continuous period of free time during the event.
The relative order of all the meetings should stay the same and they should remain non-overlapping.
Return the maximum amount of free time possible after rearranging the meetings.
Note that the meetings can not be rescheduled to a time outside the event.
 
Example 1:

Input: eventTime = 5, k = 1, startTime = [1,3], endTime = [2,5]
Output: 2
Explanation:

Reschedule the meeting at [1, 2] to [2, 3], leaving no meetings during the time [0, 2].

Example 2:

Input: eventTime = 10, k = 1, startTime = [0,2,9], endTime = [1,4,10]
Output: 6
Explanation:

Reschedule the meeting at [2, 4] to [1, 3], leaving no meetings during the time [3, 9].

Example 3:

Input: eventTime = 5, k = 2, startTime = [0,1,2,3,4], endTime = [1,2,3,4,5]
Output: 0
Explanation:
There is no time during the event not occupied by meetings.

 
Constraints:

1 <= eventTime <= 10^9
n == startTime.length == endTime.length
2 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= k <= n
0 <= startTime[i] < endTime[i] <= eventTime
endTime[i] <= startTime[i + 1] where i lies in the range [0, n - 2].

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maxFreeTime(self, eventTime: int, k: int, startTime: List[int], endTime: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's craft code:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxFreeTime(self, eventTime: int, k: int, startTime: List[int], endTime: List[int]) -> int:
        gaps = [startTime[0]]
        for i in range(1, len(startTime)):
            gaps.append(startTime[i] - endTime[i-1])
        gaps.append(eventTime - endTime[-1])
        
        window = k + 1
        cur = sum(gaps[:window])
        ans = cur
        for i in range(window, len(gaps)):
            cur += gaps[i] - gaps[i - window]
            if cur > ans:
                ans = cur
        return ans
```

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxFreeTime(self, eventTime: int, k: int, startTime: List[int], endTime: List[int]) -> int:
        gaps = [startTime[0]]
        for i in range(1, len(startTime)):
            gaps.append(startTime[i] - endTime[i - 1])
        gaps.append(eventTime - endTime[-1])

        w = k + 1
        current = sum(gaps[:w])
        ans = current
        for i in range(w, len(gaps)):
            current += gaps[i] - gaps[i - w]
            if current > ans:
                ans = current
        return ans
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maxFreeTime(self, eventTime: int, k: int, startTime: List[int], endTime: List[int]) -> int:
        gaps = [startTime[0]]
        for i in range(1, len(startTime)):
            gaps.append(startTime[i] - endTime[i - 1])
        gaps.append(eventTime - endTime[-1])

        window = k + 1
        current = sum(gaps[:window])
        ans = current

        for i in range(window, len(gaps)):
            current += gaps[i] - gaps[i - window]
            if current > ans:
                ans = current

        return ans
```
